Chapter 12Educational transformations for IoMT forensicsChallenges and opportunities

Soufiane Ouariach, Fatima Zahra Ouariach, Inam Ullah Khan, Mariya Ouaissa, and Mariyam Ouaissa

DOI: 10.1201/9781003640325-12

12.1 Introduction

Healthcare technology development has led to an unmatched convergence of medical machines, the internet, and therefore established the Internet of Medical Things (IoMT) infrastructure. The technological fusion between medical devices and the internet enables healthcare progress but raises serious difficulties in maintaining security and privacy together with forensic requirements. Medical devices that connect to the internet are steadily becoming common in healthcare spaces, which necessitates expert forensic knowledge to protect healthcare system integrity and security.

The specialized field of IoMT forensics combines elements from digital forensics as well as medical technology with cybersecurity disciplines. IoMT forensics combines the necessary approaches, instruments, and specialized knowledge needed to examine security vulnerabilities and data corruption and unauthorized access in IoMT networks. Medical device investigations remain challenging because they involve healthcare data sensitivity combined with life-threatening security failures that occur within this medical domain [1, 2]. The field of IoMT forensics gains importance although current educational programs fail to match essential skills needed by professionals in this domain.

The technical complexity of IoMT systems along with ethical healthcare data investigation aspects present challenges for educational institutions to create sufficient curricula [3, 4]. The fast-moving technological landscape makes it challenging for training programs because new devices together with protocols and security concerns must be added to educational content [5]. Current training programs in forensic practice often lack sufficient practical content regarding real-world implementations of IoMT forensic technology. While theoretical knowledge is important, it frequently fails to align with operational requirements in research facilities and investigation sites [5]. Hands-on training with modern technologies, including machine learning systems and virtual reality for crime investigation, has become essential for preparing graduates for professional practice [6].

Researchers study the present condition of IoMT forensic education alongside the necessary skills and innovative teaching approaches to meet these requirements in this chapter. The analysis reviews expert training challenges in IoMT forensics by identifying two main shortcomings that result from insufficient instruction about medical technology integration with forensic science and limited training capacity compared to industrial demands. IoMT forensic scientists need three main areas of competency consisting of technical expertise, medical knowledge, regulatory standards, and interdisciplinary subjects according to the defined outlines. The assessment evaluates new educational approaches that combine problem-based learning (PBL) with hands-on activities and virtual laboratories and university–industry cooperative programs. The text examines sustainable barriers to IoMT forensics training through evaluations of rapidly changing technology alongside ethical and sustainability demands and training program accessibility challenges. This chapter evaluates successful training models while offering perspective on continuous adaptive educational strategies which generate valuable information for developing effective strategies in the dynamic field.

12.2 Current panorama of IoMT forensics training

12.2.1 Current situation: curricular gaps and shortcomings

The current state of IoMT forensics education showcases significant gaps in the curricula of many forensic science programs. IoMT forensics faces two main difficulties due to fast technology growth and its specific requirements for using forensic science to investigate connected medical devices.

The education of IoMT forensics faces a critical deficiency because universities offer insufficient training on both medical technology integration and forensic science methodologies. The fast-growing deployment of IoMT devices creates new security weaknesses that allow botnet attackers to damage healthcare system data confidentiality and integrity and cause system unavailability. The educational curricula fail to integrate enough computer science principles while missing necessary cybersecurity measures that protect against vulnerabilities [1, 2]. Education curricula need a structured design to incorporate complete training about identifying cyber dangers, together with network vulnerabilities analysis, along with traditional forensic science subjects. Basic technology subjects that integrate into forensic science curriculum programs prepare students to tackle the particular difficulties associated with IoMT devices.

The present educational approach fails to include interdisciplinary knowledge in its teaching programs. Students need to grasp both advanced technological concepts and forensic principles to perform successful IoMT forensic investigations while learning data analysis methods through artificial intelligence and evidence management using blockchain technology [3, 4]. Educational programs should integrate these domains because they produce specific applications and effects during forensic investigations. The implementation of blockchain provides forensic practitioners the ability to protect evidence authenticity while making data across systems more reliable, thus showing needed advancements in forensic procedures [3].

Current training programs dedicated to forensic practice lack sufficient content coverage of practical aspects when it comes to IoMT forensic technology implementations in real-life situations. Theoretical understandings normally fail to connect with actual operational needs that occur within research facilities and investigation sites [5]. Graduates from forensic programs gain increased readiness through hands-on training related to innovative technologies including data analysis using machine learning systems and virtual reality tools for solving crime investigations [6]. Students must complete practical training because it prepares them to become skilled in using modern forensic technologies and methodologies in their professional practice.

The main issue today is the absence of standard accreditation criteria for IoMT forensics educational programs. Different educational establishments have chosen diverse training methods for this new field, but consistent standards and benchmarks are essential for future development. Specific accreditation procedures designed for IoMT forensics education will establish baseline competency standards that guarantee that all forensic science graduates demonstrate essential proficiency in handling complex technological issues in forensic applications [5].

The educational system requires immediate attention to fill existing curriculum gaps regarding IoMT forensic training. Educational improvement in IoMT forensics can be achieved through interdisciplinary learning, practical training, and standardized accreditation standards. Educational approaches must adapt to technological advancements in forensic science because this ensures forensic professionals can handle the challenges posed by IoMT.

12.2.2 Fracture between industrial requirements and training offers

A wide difference exists between current industry requirements and available training programs for professionals who work with IoMT technology, which threatens effective IoMT implementation in healthcare systems. Rapid technological changes along with the demands of the healthcare industry form the core elements that create this training deficit.

Many educational programs fail to demonstrate the complex multidisciplinary requirements of IoMT devices. During interconnection, multiple healthcare devices and systems form the IoMT framework which demands individual security protocols for each component. Research shows medical organizations need customized security solutions to protect their IoMT devices because standard cybersecurity methods prove insufficient for healthcare-specific security needs [7, 8]. Graduate education programs fail to focus on security protocols alongside risk assessment methods and effective intrusion detection technologies because of this their students lack industry readiness [9, 10].

The current educational programs lack sufficient training on real-world implementations and practical applications related to IoMT. Students need practical application of theoretical knowledge to develop operational competencies that suit the evolving environment of connected medical devices. The expanding use of wearable technologies and remote monitoring systems demands professionals to obtain an understanding of machine learning and data analysis methods for tracking patient conduct and spotting abnormal patterns [11, 12]. Students who lack practical training fail to develop critical thinking skills, which they need to handle real-life professional challenges. The educational system often maintains an inadequate connection with industry professionals, which leads to accommodating contemporary market requirements in curriculum development. The pace of IoMT technology evolution outpaces educational institution efforts to update their programs with emerging developments. Graduates face difficulty because of a lack of connection between their education and the expectations of potential employers [10, 13]. Academia alongside industry partners should jointly create educational curricula that focus on current market trends while matching their output to exact professional requirements.

The constantly changing nature of IoMT requires ongoing learning yet professional training programs fail to highlight the need for continuous education and skills development. Professionals working in IoMT need to pursue lifelong education because both technical and regulatory environments keep evolving so they can maintain their understanding of best practices together with compliance requirements and IoT technology developments [14]. Training should provide essential learning practices that encourage personnel to pursue self-directed education and career advancement. The current mismatch between professional requirements and existing training programs for IoMT exists due to insufficient practical focus, insufficient curriculum-real-world relationships, as well as insufficient ongoing educational programs. The workforce needs to be properly trained through combined collaboration between educational institutions and industry leaders and professional organizations for effective integration of IoMT in healthcare systems.

12.3 Core competencies for the IoMT forensics expert

12.3.1 Technological expertise: IoMT protocols and analysis tools

IoMT forensics experts play a critical role because they need to handle connected medical devices together with their security and investigative challenges. Managing the security challenges in the field requires professionals to develop specific essential technical competencies for effective understanding and mitigation. An expert in IoMT forensic work must develop a complete understanding of all existing protocols.

The IoMT system relies on various communication protocols that utilize standard protocols such as message queuing telemetry transport and constrained application protocol [15]. The analysis of data exchanges between medical devices becomes possible for forensic experts through their understanding of IoMT protocols, which help them investigate ecosystem interactions. The expertise of investigators must include knowledge of secure communication protocols because weaknesses in these security systems lead to major health data breaches and unauthorized access to sensitive medical information [16]. The analysis of IoT technology demands a complete understanding of standard IoT protocols and specialized healthcare-oriented protocols.

The investigation of IoMT devices requires complete technical competence with multiple analytical tools. The fast-growing cyber forensic tool capabilities that extract data from IoMT devices need experts who understand how to operate such tools specifically for intrusion detection and malware analysis. To conduct effective IoMT, forensics experts must have the capability to analyze intricate datasets obtained from these devices [17]. Forensic experts gain improved effectiveness through the use of statistical and analytical software because it helps identify data patterns with anomalies and potential security incidents. The growing importance of advanced technologies for healthcare analysts includes machine learning and blockchain system knowledge. Healthcare professionals are starting to use blockchain technology to protect the security and integrity of data within IoMT networks [18]. A forensic expert working in IoMT should master blockchain technology to both protect evidence integrity and validate transmission data integrity. Machine learning algorithms perform a vital role when it comes to detecting anomalies automatically and predicting security breaches within IoMT systems [19]. The comprehension of these modern technological domains allows forensic practitioners to maximize their use of the advanced detection and investigation methods effectively.

For effective incident response, IoMT forensics experts must also develop skills related to cybersecurity practices that ensure device and data security. Given the escalating threats faced by IoMT devices, knowledge of best practices in network security, including penetration testing and ethical hacking, is essential [20]. This expertise is necessary not only for protecting the integrity of medical data but also for conducting thorough forensic investigations when security incidents occur.

12.3.2 Medical and regulatory expertise: HIPAA, RGPD, and industry standards

The practice of IoMT forensics demands complete medical understanding along with regulatory expertise from professionals. This expertise encompasses familiarity with pertinent legislation such as the Health Insurance Portability and Accountability Act (HIPAA) in the United States and the General Data Protection Regulation (GDPR) in Europe, as well as a deep understanding of medical standards and practices. The combination of healthcare and technology expertise allows IoMT forensics experts to maintain medical system integrity while ensuring compliance. Advanced knowledge of HIPAA stands as the initial requirement for success. The HIPAA privacy rules require healthcare providers and EHR-handling organizations to implement strict security measures for safeguarding protected patient data in the United States [21]. All forensic professionals working in IoMT must understand HIPAA regulations to determine the consequences of data breaches and unauthorized access to individual health information. Their understanding allows them to assess breaches properly so they can suggest suitable remediation steps that meet legal requirements.

The GDPR establishes extensive data protection standards throughout the European Union, thus affecting the forensic analysis of IoMT devices. Organizations must uphold individual rights regarding personal data under the GDPR because they process this information. Experts need to grasp data minimization together with purpose limitation and data subject rights principles because they determine how medical data should be handled through IoMT systems [21]. The failure to follow GDPR requirements sets off serious legal consequences which require forensic experts to include this fundamental knowledge in their investigative procedures.

The forensic analysis of IoMT requires medical standards that determine how devices conduct their functions while maintaining patient safety. The FDA in the United States and European Medicines Agency (EMA) establish both medical device approval protocols and continuous compliance and monitoring requirements for in-use devices [22]. IoMT forensics experts need to understand these standards because they determine device compliance with legal requirements for investigating device failures and deceptive activities.

Healthcare professionals must keep pace with the evolving healthcare technologies by learning about new regulations and standards that affect IoMT devices. The healthcare industry dedicates more attention to creating specialized cybersecurity solutions for medical devices because these devices become connected and exposed to external security threats. The enforcement of HIPAA privacy regulations requires detailed knowledge so healthcare professionals can establish secure methods for protecting important health information [23].

12.3.3 Interdisciplinary skills: ethics, communication, and collaboration

Essential skills for IoMT forensics experts encompass a wide range of interdisciplinary capabilities, particularly in ethics, communication, and teamwork. The interdisciplinary work environment demands these essential abilities from professionals who handle the intersection area between technology and healthcare and regulatory compliance. Ethics stands as an essential base for conducting IoMT forensic investigations. Healthcare professionals need to handle medical data with care because they encounter various ethical challenges regarding patient privacy rights together with the requirements for informed consent and proper technology utilization. The knowledge of ethical principles helps forensic experts in IoMT make proper decisions that both fulfill legal mandates and protect patient health. To handle sensitive information properly, ethical commitments must govern operations since they serve to safeguard patient rights and meet all applicable regulatory standards [24].

The success of IoMT forensic work depends on strong communication abilities. Forensic experts must deliver complicated technical information about forensic discoveries to a wide range of healthcare providers, together with legal professionals and patients. Effective communication creates trust and mutual understanding between all parties because they are essential for collaborative work in investigations [25, 26]. The conversion of complex technical results into simple terms enables IT specialists to work better with healthcare practitioners so they can understand forensic analysis implications together. Forensic experts need to develop specialized terminology which they can adjust for different forensic environments [27]. Multiple disciplines, including healthcare, information technology, and law, must collaborate for solving most challenges in forensic investigations of IoMT systems. A team-based method helps combine multiple perspectives from experts, which specifically helps solve complex problems created by IoMT deployment. The application of efficient teamwork techniques enables multiple perspectives and resources to come together for solving problems in forensic investigations. Security implementations in patient data management gain efficiency through joint efforts between healthcare providers and cybersecurity professionals who maintain compliance and ethical requirements.

Teamwork emerges as a fundamental ability in IoMT forensics since various challenges demand experts from healthcare, information technology, and law to work together. A team-oriented approach enables diverse insights and expertise to unite effectively when handling the complex implementation challenges of IoMT systems. A successful team effort allows investigators to collect numerous points of view and assets, which produces better insights during forensic examinations. Cybersecurity professionals working together with healthcare providers bring efficiency to patient data management by securing data and maintaining ethical compliance standards. Professionals who work in IoMT forensics need to develop three essential skills: ethics understanding, alongside communication and teamwork competency. Knowledge of ethical principles helps healthcare practitioners move safely through complex rules while maintaining patient privacy protection. Diverse stakeholders work together effectively due to communication skills, so they can create innovative solutions in this multidisciplinary field through teamwork. These skilled competencies of IoMT aim to create healthcare technologies that establish stronger security and ethics as well as deliver superior effectiveness.

12.4 Innovative educational paradigms

12.4.1 Problem-solving pedagogy

PBL educational models continue to grow in popularity when teaching IoMT forensics experts in different fields of study. Through PBL students develop active learning ability together with critical thinking skills when faced with real-world problems that demand their interdisciplinary collaboration to solve them. Students who implement this method develop both their technical abilities and essential interdisciplinary talents that include ethics together with communication and teamwork skills.

The primary advantage of PBL enables different academic backgrounds to work together in meaningful collaborative projects. Students who receive interdisciplinary education gain the versatility to handle multiple career options and face current workplace obstacles [28]. The integration of knowledge from technology, medicine, and ethics becomes essential for IoMT forensics because experts need to solve complex medical device and data privacy problems.

The research conducted by Scott and White confirms that PBL practices boost critical thinking abilities and problem-solving capabilities and teamworking competencies [29]. Students enrolled in IoMT programs acquire vital skills through interdisciplinary projects that enable them to combine knowledge across disciplines, thus effectively solving forensic cases. The practical learning method helps students understand complex IoMT system functions and teaches them to tackle issues through various viewpoints. Through PBL students develop ethical intelligence when they handle actual dilemmas that arise from forensic work in the IoMT field. Student encounters ethical issues regarding patient privacy together with data usage during forensic investigations, which leads to important discussions about both ethical principles and regulatory rules. The implementation of PBL methods strengthens the essential competency of effective communication. The learning design compels students to both explain their work outcomes and team up successfully with classmates who come from various cultural backgrounds. Forensic professionals need to master the ability to explain intricate findings to nonspecialist audiences because this skill directly benefits real-world implementations, especially in healthcare institutions. According to Ferns et al., graduates who experience interdisciplinary PBL develop better readiness for technology-mediated workplaces that require effective communication and collaboration [30]. The PBL framework serves as a powerful tool for developing teamwork capabilities which prove necessary for all IoMT forensics experts. Students develop teamworking abilities when they solve complex problems together because they learn to manage opinion differences and solve conflicts while using each member’s unique abilities. The collaborative environment of PBL training enables future practitioners to master team-based work that they will need in their professional careers. Students believe that integrated coursework strengthens their abilities to collaborate and solve problems [31].

The educational implementation of PBL approaches for IoMT forensics experts brings many advantageous outcomes. Training approaches like these enhance technical abilities as well as develop key interdisciplinary capabilities built upon ethics application alongside communication standards and teamwork understanding. Educational programs adopting PBL for IoMT instruction will help their graduates tackle emerging challenges in this evolving field better.

12.4.2 Hands-on training and virtual laboratories

Incorporating hands-on training and virtual laboratories into the educational experience of IoMT forensics experts is becoming increasingly essential for developing practical skills and competencies. This innovative instructional approach fosters critical learning outcomes that align with the complexities of modern healthcare technology.

Hands-on training provides students with the opportunity to engage directly with IoMT technologies, allowing them to apply theoretical knowledge in practical, real-world scenarios. Such training can take various forms, including workshops, simulations, and live projects that utilize current IoMT devices and systems. Khatib et al. [32] highlight the importance of practical applications in the IoMT realm, emphasizing that exposure to real cases can improve problem-solving skills and deepen understanding of medical devices and their implications in healthcare settings.

Furthermore, hands-on training allows for the integration of ethical discussions related to the misuse of IoMT technologies, such as data breaches and privacy violations. Addressing ethical dilemmas during practical sessions reinforces the importance of maintaining patient confidentiality and upholding the integrity of healthcare systems, thus preparing students to navigate these challenges effectively.

On the other hand, virtual laboratories serve as an educational advancement that uses technological tools to generate virtual training spaces that supplement traditional laboratory sessions. The approach provides students with simulated laboratory experiences that do not depend on physical resources or geographical constraints. Virtual labs serve as testing grounds that enable studies of different IoMT devices alongside data evaluation and forensic research under controlled settings. Bhowal et al. demonstrate that these educational environments strengthen both student competencies and teamwork abilities when students tackle complicated problems [33]. Virtual laboratories help students conduct experiments on different IoMT system scenarios which would otherwise be unavailable through traditional laboratory settings. Students who take part in simulated activities enhance their computational abilities and critical thinking skills for dealing with IoMT technology risks.

Research indicates that virtual laboratories boost student performance in IoMT system management as well as educational outcomes. The combination of simulations with virtual tools allows students to test machine learning methods and diagnostic methods that apply to IoMT disease prediction systems and patient monitoring applications [34, 35]. The ability to modify training programs according to healthcare sector developments becomes simpler because virtual labs provide flexible options to institutions. Virtual laboratories provide future forensic scientists with continuous learning and skill development through updated resources and tools, which keeps them informed about technology advancements [36].

The education of IoMT forensics experts requires hands-on training along with virtual laboratories because these methods help develop necessary competencies for dealing with emerging field challenges. The educational approach generates better technical proficiency together with moral understanding while developing essential skills needed for healthcare technology advancement.

12.4.3 University–industry synergies

The field of IoMT forensics requires increasing industry–university partnerships to advance research along with innovation and education. The partnership leads to knowledge exchange through practical training that promotes essential technological developments required to solve healthcare technology problems.

Industry–university collaboration in IoMT enables the productive relationship between theoretical research and practical application as its main advantage. Partnerships between academic institutions and industrial organizations have made major contributions to medical device development along with clinical research [37]. Joint funding opportunities from these collaborations let universities access essential resources that they would not have access to otherwise. The collaborative space supports students and faculty to participate in advanced medical research which directly impacts healthcare practice.

The collaboration between these organizations results in the development of new IoMT solutions that address particular healthcare requirements. The combined knowledge of product developers alongside medical researchers demonstrates the benefit for rapid point-of-care testing devices [38]. The collaboration between researchers creates valuable opportunities that yield significant positive results in healthcare innovation.

The development of capable IoMT forensics professionals depends on practical training, which partnerships between industry and universities help to supply. The effective connections between universities and enterprises described by Li and Niyomsilp show how to improve graduate competence through industry-based curriculum changes and knowledge transfer [39].

Additionally, internships, cooperative education, and project-based learning initiatives enable students to apply their academic knowledge to real-world situations, bridging the gap between education and industry demands. This practical experience is invaluable for IoMT students who must navigate complex regulatory environments, technical challenges, and ethical considerations that arise in the deployment of medical technologies.

Students and faculty who participate in industry partnerships develop professional relationships through networking, which guides their career development. The paper of Ragupathy et al. emphasizes that universities maintain their status as essential innovation centers by connecting students with industry experts who share career guidance for IoMT pathways [40]. The mentoring process helps students gain workforce readiness while developing their professional self-assurance before entering professional positions.

12.4.4 MOOCs and online learning resources

Massive open online courses (MOOCs) have revolutionized educational practices while training professionals to work in IoMT forensics. Interactive online education through MOOCs provides convenient and wide-ranging educational materials as essential elements to boost educational performance in rapidly transforming fields of study. The primary benefit that MOOCs provide to learners stems from their adaptable format and broad reach. Education through MOOCs allows students to join courses whenever they want and from any location, thus eliminating traditional learning obstacles. The MOOC framework developed by Ismail et al. created a beneficial learning environment where students accessed online resources easily through smartphone devices according to Ismail et al. [41]. The open structure of MOOCs serves professionals well since they can pursue their careers and personal obligations while continuing their education. Higher accessibility to quality education occurs due to MOOCs’ reduced cost structure. MOOCs provide basic education to students without cost or minimal payment, which ensures equal learning opportunities for students across different economic backgrounds. Educational opportunities for all can produce a better-educated workforce in the IoMT sector, which will be better prepared with the necessary knowledge and skills.

The vast assortment of courses available through MOOCs creates opportunities to enrich the learning process for students. The popularity of MOOCs continues to rise through multiple academic disciplines such as health and medicine [42]. Online videos used as educational materials help students stay more engaged in their studies. IoMT forensics professionals can access diverse learning resources that provide knowledge about data analytics and cybersecurity practices together with ethical data handling methods to maintain field-level awareness. Participating in MOOCs enables students to take advantage of peer learning and network-building opportunities because of their collaborative framework. Students who take part in discussion forums and group projects at MOOCs exchange valuable ideas and perspectives that help them develop essential interdisciplinary skills needed for IoMT forensics.

Through MOOC integration with simulations combined with interactive content, students gain improved practical training capabilities. Hendriks et al. [43] state that medical MOOCs of high-quality use multiple instructional approaches including interactive student activities to help students understand and remember the material better. Interactive learning modules provide essential value to IoMT forensic education because they enable students to conduct digital forensic investigations in simulated conditions. Students using MOOCs can access virtual laboratories which offer them the chance to practice laboratory experiments remotely. The practical competencies needed for effective work in IoMT settings require hands-on training since professionals must master different technologies and tools.

Through their design, MOOCs create an environment for people to learn continuously as well as advance their professional skills. The field of IoMT requires this aspect because technology and regulatory landscapes continue to change in this domain. Through MOOCs, professionals gain access to the most recent developments and best practices as well as tools that are relevant to IoMT forensic investigations. Azami and Ibrahim [44] explain how MOOCs function as a modern learning method that enables continuous educational prospects. MOOCs and online learning resources serve as an effective educational system for IoMT forensics professionals. These online learning platforms offer crucial benefits such as ease of accessibility, flexible scheduling, diverse educational content, hands-on learning possibilities and ongoing education capabilities, which make them essential tools for training future forensic experts. Education institutions can improve professional training through these platforms to handle the complex healthcare challenges resulting from IoMT technology integration.

12.5 Specific challenges for sustainable training in IoMT forensics

The education of IoMT forensics experts encounters multiple distinctive hurdles because of quick technological changes, medical data ethics, along with sustainability issues, and challenges with training program access and inclusivity. The success of professionals who work with IoMT systems depends on solving these essential challenges in the modern, rapidly changing landscape.

The rapid technological advancements pose a major obstacle when training professionals in IoMT forensics. New IoMT devices and protocols develop at an overwhelming rate that causes difficulties for institutions that want their training curricula to stay relevant. Educators need to update their programs regularly to demonstrate new IoMT technology developments alongside their effects on forensic methods [45]. The utilization of out-of-date instructional materials creates deficient learning environments because it prepares students who face modern workplace demands like protecting against cyberattacks and following technology compliance requirements during deployment. The speed of technological advancements requires a learning system that develops lifelong learning abilities and adaptability skills in professionals. The availability of MOOCs and online learning resources enables professionals to continuously update their education through flexible, up-to-date content, which helps decrease this challenge. The resources enable practitioners to learn about new technologies as they emerge through just-in-time learning methods [46].

The training of specialists who examine IoMT systems faces obstacles because of the ethical requirements for dealing with confidential medical information. Because IoMT deals with extensive personal health information collection and transmission, it demands strict compliance with HIPAA and GDPR [47]. The unauthorized access to sensitive healthcare information leads to both breaches of patient privacy and negatively impacts healthcare deliverables. Apprehensive forensics experts need comprehensive training, that teaches about privacy laws and ethical implications their profession entails. Real-world ethical situations encountered in IoMT environments should be studied through case examples to improve educational experiences by teaching students how to handle complex moral challenges.

The ongoing technological progress of IoMT devices raises severe environmental preservation challenges. IoMT technology produces major environmental effects because of its manufacturing process, distribution methods, and its end-of-life disposal phase creates electronic waste and depletes resources. Training programs must include modules that teach students about sustainable development practices for IoMT technologies. The healthcare sector depends on future professionals who receive education about sustainable medical device development life cycles as well as recycling practices to achieve responsible technological progress. The awareness of ethical duties by IoMT forensics experts supports worldwide efforts to decrease environmental impacts [48].

Training programs that are not widely accessible create difficulties in developing IoMT forensics professionals. Traditional training and higher education programs face accessibility challenges because of geographical, socioeconomic, and technological obstacles that prevent potential students from participating. Institutions need to establish online platforms and combination learning models to make important educational content accessible equally to all students. Online education through MOOCs enables educational expansion because it distributes current learning content to students from various social backgrounds without requiring location-based or budgetary limitations [45]. Programs designed for different learning styles should include accessible resources, which ensure students with any ability level or heritage gain benefits from their IoMT forensics training. The sustainable practice of IoMT forensics training faces four unique barriers from technological change speed to ethical and environmental issues, and training access limitations, which require solution-oriented educational development approaches. Flexible training approaches combined with ethical curriculum integration together with sustainability practices and expanded accessibility allow institutions to develop future forensic experts who understand the advancing field challenges.

12.6 Case studies of successful training programs in IoMT forensics

Different international training programs for IoMT forensics have emerged as examples of best practice through which effective methods have been developed and significant lessons have been learned. This section examines successful training approaches together with participant feedback systems and performance evaluation methods.

The Medical Education Partnership Initiative launched an African program to improve medical education systems because Africa faced a healthcare worker shortage. This initiative successfully built workforce capacity by integrating clinical education with community health, which enables personnel to address various health problems including those from IoMT [49]. The MEPI program focused on community-based training, which let medical students connect with healthcare institutions to put their theoretical knowledge into practical application. Through this approach, medical students gained deeper educational value which simultaneously produced better health results for their communities.

Training of Trainers (ToT) serves as a GHP initiative model that teaches sustainable health systems through local educator training within low- and middle-income countries. Structured training of local healthcare workers produces positive outcomes with high-quality results and optimizes resource usage within the public health sector [50]. Training sessions combine fundamental skills training with ethical instructions, which prepares trainers to deliver knowledge to others in a sustainable manner. Talib et al. [51] performed a scoping review that evaluated Sub-Saharan Africa PGME programs from 1992 through 2017 to explore their development and overcome challenges in a systematic manner. The study demonstrated the need for both global alliances together with faculty involvement to keep training programs complete and relevant. The research demonstrates that educational partnerships create better learning experiences, which leads to improved health workforce ability to tackle local medical problems.

The training programs received feedback from their participants which revealed key themes. Through the MEPI initiative, the training program demonstrated successful curriculum adaptation by changing its strategies when health needs evolved [49]. Training content remains relevant through continuous updates of educational materials. Healthcare technology advancement demands focused ethical training for all professionals. Training participants developed enhanced patient privacy and data security awareness through the addition of ethical discussions into their sessions according to feedback [50]. The development of competent IoMT forensic professionals requires adopting ethical frameworks as a fundamental practice. Participants from GHP and MEPI programs emphasized the importance of networking with mentors and peers through which professionals develop productive partnerships to share resources [51]. A community of practice formed among trainees enables them to learn from each other and develop their skills.

The evaluation of these training programs requires assessments to measure three key aspects: participant satisfaction, as well as knowledge retention and practical competency development.

The MEPI program achieved both higher medical education enrollment numbers and better student learning results and satisfaction scores [49]. The participants who answered the feedback surveys said they gained capabilities to use their acquired knowledge in practical real-world contexts.

Healthcare education programs using competency assessments strengthened both patient care skills and graduate abilities to transform regional healthcare delivery systems. The research demonstrated that professionals who received training showed better competence in IoMT device management as well as ethical implications which resulted in better healthcare practices [51].

The GHP and similar programs conduct extended alumni monitoring that tracks both professional development and ongoing healthcare involvement within community settings. The long-term tracking demonstrates that training results are sustainable by showing how trained individuals drive healthcare model evolution [50].

Successful training programs for IoMT forensics exist throughout the world through collaborative partnerships and adaptive learning systems. The case studies deliver important findings that future program development should adopt alongside ethical training and structured curriculum and networking support to build capable and ethical IoMT forensics practitioners.

12.7 Strategies for continuous and adaptive education

The evolution of IoMT forensic practice necessitates adapting education for experts because of emerging medical technology. The competency of IoMT forensics experts depends heavily on three key strategies: continuing professional development, communities of practice, and self-learning resources, and adapting to emerging technological developments. IoMT professionals must participate in continuing professional development programs because these prepare them to utilize modern technologies and practices in their field. Lifelong learning becomes possible through CPD because it provides professionals with current information and new abilities.

Online courses along with workshops enable practitioners to gain enhanced knowledge about new technologies, ethical aspects, and regulatory guidelines. [52]. The CPD programs develop both personal progress and organizational achievement in workforce competence levels. Structured programs with practical applications within CPD enable professionals to experience firsthand scenarios that relate to IoMT forensic work. Real-world experiences complement theoretical learning to teach practitioners how their work functions in practice [53]. Medical technology workshops that simulate breach scenarios together with ethical dilemmas allow professionals to apply learned knowledge in realistic settings to develop their decision-making abilities.

Through Communities of practice (CoPs) and learning networks, professionals can access environments that facilitate the exchange of knowledge and experiences alongside best practices. Professional development receives enhancement through networks that provide collaborative and mentorship services. IoMT forensics experts who participate in CoPs maintain their connection to industry trends and innovations since members routinely provide essential information about current technologies and field-specific challenges [54]. Members of CoPs use structured interactions to develop solutions together for the common challenges that appear in IoMT forensics. The shared knowledge among professionals provides maximum benefit when technology advances rapidly because members can draw from each other’s practical experiences [54].

Self-directed learning resources that include MOOCs with webinars and online tutorials provide professionals with a flexible opportunity to determine their learning timeline. These resources provide accessible and flexible learning opportunities that enable practitioners to fill particular knowledge deficits while developing skills needed for their work responsibilities. Self-directed learning models serve as essential components because they match educational materials with personal objectives, thus creating individualized learning experiences [53–56]. Interactive simulations combined with video tutorials improve both student interest and their ability to keep information in their memory. The approach serves both student learning diversity and professional content review by allowing professionals to access information multiple times for better understanding of complex IoMT forensic concepts [57].

Educational programs need to implement adaptable methods because the IoMT sector experiences fast technological development. New technologies such as artificial intelligence and machine learning, alongside cybersecurity techniques, are integrated into education curricula to teach professionals skills for modern challenges [58]. Educators need to revise their training content and teaching approaches regularly because new technological developments appear in the market. Educational programs that implement virtual laboratories together with simulation software enable students to explore new technology through managed practice spaces. Students gain confidence in real-world scenarios use of advanced tools and techniques through practical learning experiences that promote theoretical concept application [59].

The approaches for continuous and adaptive education in IoMT forensics consist of a combination of continuing professional development, communities of practice, and self-learning resources, and technological adaptations. The advancement of continual learning practices enables professionals to maintain their expertise and intelligence in an evolving field, thus helping to improve both practices and innovations in IoMT forensic work [60].

12.8 Conclusion

The chapter highlights the necessity for educational transformation which will produce professionals capable of handling IoMT forensics. The existing frameworks are lacking certain elements, combining IoMT technical knowledge with medical regulations and interdisciplinary capabilities is indispensable. The authors introduce PBL and virtual labs, along with university–industry collaborations, as strategies that will develop these competencies. Numerous obstacles, including quick technology evolution and moral dilemmas, together with ensuring the inclusion of all parties, remain persistent. The development of sustainable education requires adaptive curricula combined with professional development, along with institutional partnerships between healthcare providers and regulators, to create prepared IoMT forensic experts who can handle changing healthcare security challenges.
